mr president, today the swedish delegation voted in favour of the report on intra-eu solidarity, because we do support burden sharing and mutual assistance among all member states. however, the compromise reached is not balanced. first of all our work on asylum has to be based on correct facts. although the report is based on a notion that only countries on the external border are subject to extreme pressure and that they are the ones receiving the largest number of asylum seekers, this is not the reality. asylum seekers enter europe not only by sea, and second-line countries – where their final destination is – should also be taken into account. i would just like to take an example. in sweden, we are now receiving a thousand people from syria every week and of course we feel enormous solidarity towards them. so external borders and absolute numbers cannot be the only criteria for receiving support and financial aid. it should also be clearly stated that relocation should be on a voluntary basis for the member states who want to participate in the programmes and of course the people concerned should be asked for their views.
mr president, today we have approved an important step towards the completion of the single market. this is the most effective way to promote growth in europe at a time of economic crisis, so we should not underestimate the importance of this legislation. although technical, it is crucial for our companies and consumers in their daily lives. in a rapidly changing world, standards must ensure interoperability and keep pace with technological developments. we also need to have effective tools to drive innovation and boost the competitiveness of european companies in global markets. i therefore welcome the fact that standards will be continue to be bottom-up, market driven and voluntary. we have adopted a framework that will help develop european standards. we have been pressing hard for better, more flexible regulation, and we are removing unnecessary administrative burdens and increasing transparency. i think it is important that this will really facilitate the participation of smes and ensure access to user-friendly standards for all. i hope we will continue to work together to implement all the aspects of the single market act effectively.
in writing. - i voted today in favour of two reports on the use of the european globalisation adjustment fund. since 2009, i have, as a rule, abstained or voted against all proposals to use the fund. this is because i do not think that the fund is the right instrument to help workers affected by structural change. the fund has now grown, evolved and made its impact. it is and will be for the foreseeable future. in future, i will now take a stand on each individual case if it meets the very strict requirements for co-financing and long-term employment measures imposed on applicants, as the two cases in question do. so it's about making the best of the situation.
in writing. - we voted today in favour of two reports on the use of the european globalisation adjustment fund. since the fund was set up in 2006, we have always abstained from voting, because we were against the fund from the beginning, as we do not believe that the fund is the right instrument to help workers affected by structural changes. the fund has now grown, evolved and received its own allocation. it exists and will not disappear for the foreseeable future. we are therefore now taking a stand on each individual case if they meet the very strict requirements for co-financing and long-term employment measures imposed on applicants. in these two cases, we consider that the applications meet these requirements. we continue to support more effective and long-term instruments to increase employment, address structural change and increase labour market flexibility in the context of the 2013 budget and the long-term budget.
in writing. − we voted today in favour of the triantaphyllides report on intra-eu solidarity, because we support burden sharing and mutual assistance among all member states in the field of asylum. however, the compromise reached could be more balanced. while the report is based on the notion that only countries on the external border are subject to extreme pressure and that they receive the largest number of asylum seekers, this is not the reality. asylum seekers enter europe not only by sea; countries of final destination – like sweden – should be taken into account as well. the external borders and absolute numbers of asylum seekers should not be the only criteria in order to receive more support and financial aid. relocation among member states should be voluntary for member states and should concern only people that have already been granted asylum, with their consent, and not asylum seekers. as we work together to achieve a common asylum system by 2012, it is important that we base our legislative work on facts with a balanced and inclusive approach.

in writing. the european parliament has adopted a resolution on the situation in the middle east. the aim is to be able to provide additional assistance in those countries which currently need additional assistance. it is also important to emphasise the responsibilities of individual countries. europe represents for many people the dream of a life in peace, freedom and security. it is therefore a matter of people's desire to realise these fundamental values when asylum policy is discussed. it is therefore regrettable that these people are repeatedly referred to as problems and 'burdens', as is done in this report. it is also urgent to stress that each country, for purely humanitarian reasons, should have the right to show radical solidarity and that the talk of harmonisation should not mean that a country withdraws or does not feel justified in humanity. we should call on all countries to pursue an asylum policy which respects human rights and freedoms. despite some problematic wording, i chose to vote in favour of the report.
in writing. - i voted in favour of the report on the electronic identification of bovine animals because it aims to reduce bureaucracy and the administrative burden on farmers and because modern technology guarantees greater food safety. the use of the new electronic labelling of animals will make it easier to recall or check infected meat quickly through the databases being set up. at the same time, i think it is good that farmers will have the option of using the electronic labelling. it involves costs and should not be something they are directly forced to do.
in writing. - the proposal for a regulation on the protection of the environment against the dangers of ionizing radiation; the particularly strict rules on sulphur emissions in the exposed seas of northern europe (the baltic sea, the north sea and the english channel) put many of the industries in sweden and other northern european countries at a major competitive disadvantage compared to the rest of europe, where higher sulphur emission limits apply, due to higher shipping costs. reducing sulphur emissions in our seas is an environmental imperative, and one to which all 27 eu member states have already committed themselves by signing the marpol convention and its annex vi. in my view, it would be better if the seas around all the eu countries were subject to the same low sulphur emission limit values as the seas in northern europe. this would not only make competition more equal and the internal market more integrated, but also clean water and air along all the eu's coasts.
in writing. the commission is also proposing to extend the scope of the directive to include the use of the term 'common carrier' to cover all transport services. we do not want european taxpayers' money to be used for illogical subsidies which distort the market mechanisms and which, in this case, also have a direct price-reducing effect on the sale of alcoholic beverages.
in writing. - the imi is a vital instrument for the internal market. cooperation between public authorities via imi contributes to the proper implementation of internal market rules and to the increased mobility of citizens and businesses in the eu. in times of crisis, a well-functioning internal market is indeed the highway to more growth, competitiveness and jobs in europe. the imi system for administrative cooperation between national authorities has been in place since 2008, but until now it lacked a legal framework. i welcome the fact that we have brought together existing practices in a single text, which increases transparency and legal certainty. at the same time, we have simplified procedures and strengthened the protection of personal data. i have, among other things, promoted the principle that data should not be stored in the system for longer than necessary, but that one should also be able to demand access to one's data for specific evidentiary purposes even after a case has been closed. as head of the ppe group, i have worked for a safe, cost-effective, flexible and user-friendly system. i particularly welcome the fact that the imi system, currently used for the services directive and vocational qualifications, will in future be able to be extended to other areas of eu legislation, such as solvit and e-commerce, on the basis of pilot projects from the commission.
in writing. - we swedish social democrats are clearly in favour of a strong welfare state financed by solid tax revenues. we therefore see significant problems in the trend towards lower corporate taxes driven by the increasingly fierce tax competition between member states. if this process is not stopped, not only will corporate taxation be eroded. it will also be difficult for those countries which want to maintain relatively high levels of taxation to be able to do so in practice. consequently, we see value in a proper debate on limiting or ending this tax competition. however, we cannot support the establishment of a minimum level of corporate taxation. it would mean that the eu was making progress in an area direct taxation where the starting point must be that the member states decide entirely on their own.
in writing. - we voted today in favour of the resolution on the commission's work programme for 2013. the main priorities for the commission in 2013 shall be to address the crisis through measures for a functioning internal market, increased competitiveness, open borders and investments in innovation, research and job creation. that is why we also voted in favour of the amendments which implied better budgetary discipline, a clearer subsidiarity principle, a stronger internal market, investment in innovation, research and enlargement. for the same reasons, we voted against the points in which the european parliament expressed its support for taxation at eu level, own resources and a change in the treaty.
in writing. - i would like to make clear by this explanation of vote why it is necessary for the commission to examine the matter further. i strongly support the free movement of persons in the eu's internal market as a fundamental right for all eu citizens, but see a need to examine the relationship between different regulatory frameworks in order for it to work in the best way. the directive on free movement gives eu citizens the right to stay in another eu country without working for more than three months if they have sufficient resources not to be considered a burden on the social assistance system of that country and are insured for health care. in several eu countries, however, a person who is not working may still be entitled to various social security benefits. the commission proposal can be read as meaning that a person who is not working and who has been granted a social security benefit (not linked to gainful employment) can be considered to have the resources to be considered not to be a burden on the social assistance system of the member state and thus to meet the requirements for the right of residence. in practice, this means that a person is first examined for entitlement to social security benefits (the regulation on the coordination of social security systems) and then for the right of residence (the directive on the free movement of persons). logically, on the contrary, the right of residence should be tested before social security benefits are granted.
in writing. - we moderates voted today for the report on the role of women in the green economy. the report includes a paragraph calling on member states to introduce quotas to increase gender equality in society. we moderates voted against this paragraph because we believe that it should be up to the member states themselves to decide how they want to achieve a society of equality, with reference to the principle of subsidiarity. furthermore, we do not believe that quotas are the right way to increase the proportion of women in senior positions.
in writing. - in today's vote on the report on the role of women in the green economy, i voted in favour of the final vote, despite voting against in most of the votes on separate items. i would like to make a reservation in particular to paragraph 35 on the gender quota, which in my view is not the right way to a truly equal society, whether it be in the appointment of political or business positions.
in writing. the report also calls for the establishment of a european network of national experts in the field of health and safety at work. the report includes a paragraph on the maternity leave directive and an invitation to the council to adopt the directive. we moderates oppose this, because the directive is a serious restriction of the principle of subsidiarity. we believe that maternity leave is a matter best decided by the member states themselves.
in writing. - we voted today in favour of the own-initiative report on the distribution of audiovisual works on the internet, as it is important to make the sector more competitive by simplifying and improving licensing and remuneration issues. the commission is also proposing to extend the scope of the directive to include the protection of the environment and the environment from harmful substances. the aim is to find a simple and clear regulatory framework that promotes free movement and makes the sector more competitive, while maintaining a balance between the protection of copyright and privacy.
madam president, i would like to thank you. this report calls for clearer political priorities and strategic lines, and i agree. but at the same time, i lack a single approach to the same problem. we cannot, for example, advocate territorial integrity and the end of occupation for some countries, while ignoring the same in other countries. the same approach must be applied to all. it is shameful that a country which is committed to a partnership with the eu occupies the territory of another country, and it is even worse that a number of resolutions on the withdrawal of the occupying forces are not respected. the second point i would like to stress is a clearer leadership towards the balkans. many of the balkan countries do not have strong leadership, and they rightly expect us to show it. we can't make the same mistake we made in the early '90s. we cannot only mediate, we must also lead. otherwise, our position and the entire enlargement process could be jeopardized.
madam president, i would like to thank ms ashton. today is certainly a happy day in my home country of sweden. it has been mentioned that the two reporters were released in ethiopia yesterday and are back home. this of course gives me the opportunity to ask ms ashton: have you received any new information about dawit isaak in the neighbouring country of eritrea? he has now been imprisoned without a trial in asmara, eritrea, for 4000 days and nights - nearly eleven years. do you have any new information?
rapporteur for the opinion of the committee on development. − mr president, dear colleagues, we are approaching a real crunch time in the debate over the future common fisheries policy, and the question is: will it be a success, or will it continue to be a disaster? at the moment, i see two real, major threats that could destroy all the good work that has been done by the rapporteurs, mr haglund, mr salavrakos, mr stevenson and also ms rodust, and the extremely long work and analyses carried out by the commission and the court of auditors. the first of these threats is paragraph 58 of the salavrakos report, which calls for fleet renewal. the eu got rid of subsidies for building new fishing vessels in the last reform in 2002, and still the fleet suffers from enormous over-capacity. it would be completely irresponsible to reintroduce this now, in the middle of the economic crisis, when we should have learnt our lesson. that lesson is that the best way to let the fishing industry become economically prosperous is to rebuild fish stocks to a level above maximum sustainable yield. this leads me to the other threat to a successful reformed fisheries policy, namely amendment 2 to the salavrakos report, which seeks to delete the word ‘above’ from ‘above msy’. that would be an extremely serious mistake. study after study has shown that if we keep stocks above msy level, the industry will enjoy greater stability and earn more money, and investments will be much safer. this is also what the pelagic rac stated in the committee on fisheries. it would give us a better marine environment and is consistent with a precautionary approach. concerning the stevenson report, we support the mandatory provision of information to consumers on the date of catch and the gear type that has been used. this should also apply for processed products. (the speaker agreed to take a blue-card question under rule 149(8))
blue-card answer. – i am actually convinced that, with just a few years of recovery of the fish stocks in europe, all the european fleets will be in a much better economic situation. i think that fishermen, as well as all other economic entrepreneurs, have the obligation to see to it that their activity is economically viable. they should be able to invest in their own renewal of their own fishing vessels. it should not be up to european taxpayers to provide for that.
for the gue/ngl group. mr president, i would like to thank you for your reply. commissioner, what is it? i would first like to thank the commission and its staff, and the council and its staff, for your contribution to the negotiation of this final result. i would also like to thank the rapporteur of this parliament, mrs antonyia parvanova, and mrs teresa jiménez-becerril barrio, for the excellent work they have done to ensure that this document has the greatest possible impact on the victims. you have fought with zeal and enthusiasm for the position of parliament. thanks again for that! as regards the proposal itself, i am particularly pleased that there is now a strong gender perspective. some examples: victims of gender-based violence are highlighted, the need for sheltered housing is identified, the right to legal information is clarified. the directive is therefore a step forward in the fight against violence by men against women. i would like to stress, however, that parliament still believes that the commission should present a proposal for an eu strategy against violence against women. only within the framework of such a coherent approach will it be possible to take effective and forceful eu action to combat all forms of violence against women in europe.
mr president, i am particularly happy that we have adopted the directive on the rights, support and protection of victims of crime, and i very much support the epp rapporteur, teresa jiménez-becerril barrio. from now on, women suffering domestic violence, children trapped in trafficking, innocent victims of terrorism and victims of all crimes will be able to have the same rights, regardless of the member state where the crime has been committed and their nationality. we have set up clear common standards to ensure that each victim is treated with respect, dignity and without discrimination across the european union. i welcome in particular the attention given to children and to people with special needs and that there will be an individual assessment of their vulnerability so as not to create first or second-class victims. in the stockholm programme we promised to have a europe that is a community of rights for all citizens. this is a significant step in that direction and i look forward to continuing to work with colleagues to make it a reality.
in writing. the european parliament has also called for the establishment of a european defence agency, which would be responsible for the coordination of the activities of the european defence agency. we believe that the report fails to address the reservations against this. we also think it would have been important to have a document which also addresses the member states' perspectives on the subject and takes account of the different traditions and positions of the member states in the fields of foreign affairs, security and defence. it is important for us to protect sweden's freedom of association.
mr president, i think it is important that we achieve better supervision and better implementation and also that we can secure the stability of european banks. i think we should be pragmatic and decisive in trying to achieve that. however, i would like to underline – and i know the commissioner does not take a different view – that when we proceed we must ensure we do not create new unions within the union, because that would lead to fragmentation which would be contrary to what we want: namely, better supervision and greater stability. so i would like to emphasise to you, mr commissioner, that we have one union, and that is the one we shall develop.
member of the commission. − mr president, some members of this house have been receiving fearsome messages about the eu being on the verge of burying 18 developing countries, and you have just heard it from the rapporteur. what you are not being told is the following. up until the end of the 1990s, the eu was granting african, caribbean and pacific countries special unilateral tariff preferences that were more generous than the generalised system of preferences. other trade partners attacked us over this in geneva. under wto rules, special preferences cannot discriminate between countries in a similar economic situation except by waiver, which we were granted for the last time between 2000 and 2007. in the mean time we were expected to bring our trade relationship into line with wto rules. in 2000 the eu and acp countries signed the cotonou agreement wherein they agreed without coercion to modernise their trade and aid relations. let us not forget that until then, even these very generous preferences were not enough to improve acp exports to europe. indeed, between 1967 and 1999 the acp’s share in total eu imports fell from 7.7 % to 2.8 %, so we agreed in cotonou – specifically in article 16 of the treaty – to conclude new bilateral trade agreements that would be wto-compatible. it was a proposal that made economic sense because of the poor experience with unilateral tariff preferences. but it was also one that matched the political moods at the time. the international community at large had finally understood that development policies which do not sustain good governance are simply not sustainable. eu trade policy had to reflect this too. i have said this before and i will say it again: the political value of bilateral trade agreements is that they create flanking policies and institutions to manage the implementation of commercial commitments. besides stimulating the two-way flow of trade, lowering customs tariffs reduces the potential for poor governance, in particular corruption. let me try once again to explain what this regulation is about. in 2007, by the time we had conducted and concluded epa negotiations with about half of the acp countries, the eu, acting in good faith, passed the market access regulation to allow our epa partners continued market access to the eu on a duty-free, quota-free basis. we did so on the understanding that our epa partners would formalise their commitment to these agreements within a year or two by signing and ratifying them. most epa partners proved us right and did take the necessary steps. eighteen had not until last year. but a few months later zimbabwe ratified its interim epa. nine of the countries left are least developed countries and will continue to enjoy duty-free, quota-free status because they continue to be eligible for ‘everything but arms’ benefits. that leaves us with only eight countries actually affected by the proposal we are discussing today. to be even clearer, we are talking about botswana, cameroon, côte d’ivoire, fiji, ghana, kenya, namibia and swaziland, which makes me wonder why these particular eight countries need more time to adjust to a situation that was already required 12 years ago, when 87 countries and territories are adjusting to our 2011 gsp proposal in less than three years. the broader picture on epas is really something to be proud of. we are continuing negotiations for comprehensive epas with practically all the epa regions and i have a small set of outstanding issues left with the east african community, with western africa and with epa partners in the southern africa development community, the sadc. furthermore we are implementing agreements concluded back in 2007 with the caribbean, papua new guinea and four eastern and southern african countries. some of them are also continuing negotiations to get to comprehensive regional agreements. what this agreement is about is the past: epas initialled by the end of 2007. so it is not about negotiations on comprehensive epas, the argument invoked by some to justify an extended deadline. this is an agreement about doing away with the past, it is not about the future. if there is such heavy resistance today from those who would prefer to extend the status quo, i cannot help but ask a question. are we not letting down a promising entrepreneurial class in eight acp countries if the 2014 deadline is extended? why should a kenyan, an ivorian or a namibian business person who has an innovative idea, finds a niche market and wishes to develop a product with the assistance of good european machinery, continue to have to pay very heavy import duties for the next two, four or even seven years? how can that business idea grow if inputs are expensive? how many new jobs could be created if tariff barriers were removed? why do we continue to see africa, the caribbean and the pacific as not being capable of exporting much more than basic agricultural commodities? that is so far from present-day realities and needs. sometimes wanting to do good is actually the enemy of the good. in conclusion, as trade commissioner-designate, i took a pledge before this parliament in february 2010 to uphold and further strengthen the multilateral trading system. at the time i also understand my ambition is to bring epa negotiations to a successful conclusion provided the results are wto-compatible and can foster development. as a commissioner i am also bound by eu law. for these reasons, as well as my conviction that free trade deals serve poor countries better, i continue to support the commission proposal to amend the market access regulation with legal effect on 1 january 2014 and i very much hope that this wisdom will also convince parliament.
for the ppe group. mr president, i would like to thank you for your reply. commissioner, what is it? for this debate to have any meaning, we need to start at the beginning with the background of why we are really discussing this. the reason is that our old trade agreement with the acp countries, that is to say the african, caribbean and pacific countries, was declared illegal. it discriminated against other poor countries. that is why we decided to embark on the path we are now negotiating with the acp countries on economic partnership agreements. it's been a long time, an unacceptably long time. it is in the interest of all of us that these agreements come into force as soon as possible. because it is only with these agreements that we can have real development in poor countries, investment, governance. the only reason we have had these temporary trade preferences is to bridge the gap that has existed between our old illegal agreements and these new economic partnership agreements. but they were just temporary. actually, the whole debate here today is about how long "temporary" is. the left here in the house says temporary means forever or at least until 2019. the socialist group says temporarily enough until 2016. i get the feeling that anytime but not right now is temporary. however, that is more than 12 years ago and it is more than temporary. why would you do that? the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council, and i am sure that the council will be able to accept them. they were not least unfair to the acp countries which respected the fact that they were temporary, negotiated and concluded partnership agreements with us. we therefore have a responsibility to put an end to this temporary arrangement. let us continue to be honest and say that we will never get any economic partnership agreements if we say that the temporary arrangement is for ever. what are we going to say to the other poor countries, to the countries that once sued us, that we don't care about either what is fair or the wto law? that is why we must support this proposal and put an end to the provisional arrangements. the ppe group therefore believes that the commission's proposal should be approved. i also think it would be good if we could try to agree to do a first reading of this to get the rules in place as soon as possible. i therefore think that the best thing we could do would be to vote on all the amendments and then postpone the final vote so that we can start negotiations with the council. it is only a date that separates us, a date that means a lot, but something i think we could agree on. i would therefore urge both the president and the rapporteur to consider whether we should not enter into negotiations with the council and try to reach an agreement at first reading.
blue-card answer. – it is quite clear; nobody is forcing anyone to sign anything. it is an offer for us to give them possibilities to negotiate epas. if they choose not to do so, they will be treated equally with other poor countries and we will be fair to them on the same basis as non-acp countries. but just because you once were part of an illegal trade agreement, you will always be a part of an illegal trade agreement: that is not fair – neither to those who sign epas nor to those who never were part of the original illegal trade agreement.
for the alde group. mr president, i would like to thank you for your reply. commissioner, what is it? a big thank you to the two rapporteurs, sophie auconie and philippe lamberts, for a good job. the alde group is in favour of both the proposals on venture capital funds and social enterprise funds. these proposals will make it easier to obtain financing for smes. it has been said here, but it is not enough to mention it once, it can be mentioned several times in view of the problems facing europe. it is also important for enhancing the competitiveness and growth potential of smes. venture capital investments can be a very valuable alternative to bank financing. to support the most promising start-ups, venture capital funds need to become larger and more diversified in their investments and to foster innovation. social entrepreneurship in the eu must be strengthened, especially in these times of crisis. we welcome the idea that managers of venture capital funds and social entrepreneurship funds may use a quality label, a european passport, to market their funds throughout the eu. it's been proven to work very well in other contexts. it is important to introduce a single rulebook throughout the eu. it'll cut down on the hassle and red tape. it facilitates investment and business in the single market. some burdensome unnecessary bureaucratic rules, such as requirements for depositaries, have been removed in the compromise with the council. i think that's good. i think we have found the right balance, and this can really facilitate investment for europe's smes. the question that remains is the question of tax havens. i hope we can work something out. i would like to conclude by saying that i find it sad that taxation issues, also in connection with the question of tax havens, cause such divisions.
for the alde group. president. - the debate is closed. i also think it is very gratifying that we have reached a settlement on this issue which enjoys wide support here in parliament and which has finally been accepted by the member states in the council. for this i would of course like to thank our rapporteur, lidia joanna geringer de oedenberg, and all the others who have worked on the report, not least alexandra thein from the liberal group. i hope and believe that the directive will be an important first step in solving the problems surrounding unauthorised works, especially in the digital age we are living in. it has been difficult to strike a good balance between safeguarding the ability of libraries and archives to make literature, including unauthorised works, available, and having reasonable rules to protect the authors who created these works, which have become unauthorised over time, but which can still offer joy and hope and a belief in the future to readers in our time. i think we have found a reasonable balance in this directive. for that i would like to sincerely thank everyone involved.
on behalf of the verts/ale group. – madam president, i am very sad to say that i am very disappointed today. this was a very good opportunity, and i think we missed it. there was a proposal from the commission, which was limited in scope and i would have wanted it to be better, but it was good. unfortunately, it got watered down, first by the processing in parliament, then in the committee and then in the trialogue. so, what we have now is a directive that, on the surface, if you read it through quickly, gives the impression or the hope that it will be helpful. i do not think it will be, in practice, because there are so many details that restrict what the museums, archives and libraries are allowed to do. there is one very good thing in this directive: it introduces the new principle of mutual recognition of orphan work status after a diligent search. that is a very good and sensible principle. this means that, for example, if it is a swedish author you only have to search in sweden – unless there are specific reasons to think that an author may be found elsewhere but, basically, for a swedish author you search in sweden, and then it is declared orphan all over the union. that is good, but where are the provisions? in the commission proposal there was an article 7 that allowed museums and libraries to do some, quite limited, searches but still to have some commercial possibilities – that was taken out. instead, what was inserted in it was that the museums and libraries will have a liability if they digitalise and make available a work and the rightholder reappears. now we all agree that, if a rightholder reappears, he or she should have the right to say: take it down, here i am, and this is mine. that is fine. but what was added also was that the museums and libraries have to pay compensation. now, the risk is that this will make the directive more or less useless anyway because, if they do not have the legal certainty, there is a big risk that they will not dare to digitise our common cultural heritage. we all want the same thing here. we would all love to see europeana filled with lots of works from every member state. unfortunately, i do not think this directive will do that. mrs gallo said that this is not the end of the road, and i want to pick up that. several other speakers have also said that this is a first step. yes, it is a first step. it is a very small step, but we will have to return to this. we have not solved the problem, and that makes me a little bit sad.
for the alde group. mr president, i would like to thank you for your reply. first of all, i would like to thank our colleague, mr sajjad karim, for the good work he has done in this report. i would like to focus these minutes on one aspect raised in the report, which is that the commission must do a better job of explaining and justifying the european added value in the legislative proposals it puts forward. i would like to emphasize that i say this as a convinced federalist, who would like to see more joint decisions and activities at european level. at the same time, we must do better at making this european added value in the work of the eu clear and easy to explain. this is not only true of our contacts with our citizens, but we must also improve our dialogue and cooperation with colleagues in the national parliaments. it is not enough that some officials of the commission and members of this house understand what the european added value of various proposals is. we must be better at listening to and explaining the views of citizens. this can lead to a better understanding of the intentions behind european legislation and in some cases also to a clearer understanding of the fact that there are not enough motives to legislate at european level. this is where the whole subsidiarity issue comes in. i hope that the commission will now take up these comments, which are particularly timely on a day like today, the day after josé manuel barroso's speech at yesterday's sitting. he stressed something new, namely that an important debate on european added value must be held at all levels. in this process i believe that the report by our colleague, mr sajjad karim, can be very helpful and clarify not only the problems but also the solutions.

in writing. - at the final vote on the abovementioned report, i, like 35% of my colleagues in parliament, abstained. the reason was that amendment no 4 was passed by a narrow majority. the amendment extends the end date of the negotiation period for the trade agreements in question from 2014 to 2016. i am convinced that it would be a positive thing for both the countries concerned and the eu if the negotiations, which should have been concluded years ago, could be concluded as soon as possible.
in writing. - i voted in favour of this report because i believe that it can be a positive step towards improving quality and facilitating both small producers and consumers. today we have protected designations of origin (pdo) and protected geographical indications (pgi) which are used to protect and enhance the quality of products with a distinctive regional character. in sweden and scandinavia we are not very good at using these terms in the way they are used in southern europe (e.g. parmaskinka, parmesan, etc.). the new proposal is more comprehensive and aims to introduce more categories and labels, for example for food from the arctic, mountain areas, etc. it can also lead to a more uniform labelling system that can be used throughout europe and that can help to strengthen swedish food exports as presented in the idea of the new food country sweden. fewer labels that consumers trust and know about make it easier to make an informed choice. the proposal also aims to facilitate administrative work and reduce red tape, which i very much welcome.

for the verts/ale group. mr president, i would like to thank you for your reply. the resolutions we are discussing here are usually about things happening outside europe's borders, but we must also discuss what has happened in hungary. but i think the focus is on the fact that when you turn people over, you usually agree to stay in prison. after a number of years, there is often a chance for a pardon or release. in this case, you choose day one to heroically pay homage to a murderer, reward him financially to exacerbate the conflict. there can be no purpose for it other than to dilute hatred and aggravate conflict. it seems that this has worked. that is why it is important now that we help armenia and azerbaijan to reach a peaceful solution to the conflict and that this kind of hate politics cannot be rewarded in any way.
mr president, i would like to thank you. a big thank you to the rapporteur and the shadow rapporteur. they have done a very good job in an extremely difficult time. very few citizens are aware that the eu budget is actually falling as a share of the member states' economies. most people think it just grows and grows. that's not true, man. it is falling despite the fact that the eu has been given new tasks and new member states. however, in the light of the economic crisis, the european parliament is very cautious in 2013. the commission's proposal is to freeze new budgetary commitments. the reason why we do not accept budget cuts is that we believe that this could worsen the crisis. the budget is in fact made up of investments that create jobs and growth and that have a solidarity-based redistributive profile, in favour of the most vulnerable countries, regions, unemployed young people and so on. in many of these countries, no job-creating investment will be made without eu support. the most controversial issue seems to be the payment appropriations. this should be a technical issue. it should be obvious that budgetary decisions must be implemented, contracts must be paid, exchange students must be paid, and so on. it is simply not acceptable that important programmes such as erasmus and the social fund now have difficulty carrying out their activities. we believe that the commission's proposals for payment appropriations for next year are not excessive but just what is needed. it is very good that commissioner lewandowski has now started to say that in earnest. finally, a few words on the european parliament's own budget. for several years now the socialist group has been pushing for savings without jeopardizing parliamentary work. we're going to continue that line now. we agree to an increase rate of 1.9%, including the costs of croatia's accession. it's actually a real budget cut. finally, we really need to make progress on the question of the seat of parliament in strasbourg. the committee on budgets has now adopted by a large majority, on my proposal among others, a more developed proposal than before, which calls for a 'road map', an action plan, to deal with the matter in practice. there are ways of resolving this with respect for history without harming the city of strasbourg and its citizens. in the long term, a solution which requires enormous costs every year and which, moreover, places a heavy burden on both the environment and the working conditions of parliament is unreasonable.
in writing. - we believe that the eu budget must be given sufficient resources to finance commitments already made. at the same time, we would like to stress that the eu must first of all adopt a restrictive approach and provide these resources by redistributing them within the existing budget. we believe that the agricultural budget should be reduced in favour of those areas which are currently facing payment problems, such as the social fund, research programmes and erasmus. we do not believe that the eu should act as an insurance company in the event of temporary crises in the agricultural sector. we welcome, however, the major cuts in eu export aid. finally, we believe it is important to make the eu's administrative expenditure more efficient and to reduce it, and we welcome the fact that the budgets of all the eu institutions except the eeas are kept below the rate of inflation. in this context, we also welcome the savings made in the travel allowances of members of the european parliament and the reduction in the appropriations for the 'house of european history' and the requirement of a single seat for the european parliament. however, work on further savings must continue.
in writing. − the 2013 budget sets out parliament's negotiating position for the negotiations with the council. the budget aims to be an instrument of economic recovery through targeted measures to boost investment, stability and growth, with particular emphasis on promoting youth employment, supporting smes and achieving the objectives of the europe 2020 strategy. the proposed budget is expansionary, which is not in keeping with calls for restraint on the part of the member states. the re-prioritization of budget lines needed is not addressed in the present proposal. the christian democrats' view of man is that every human being has an inherent inviolable value. we must devote more political energy to combating violations of such values, and this must be reflected in the eu budget. i voted in favour of proposals aimed at shifting resources from agricultural aid to internal security, i.e. the fight against crime, drug trafficking, human trafficking, and support for research and environmental protection. the core of european cooperation is more than employment levels and market competitiveness, and this must be reflected in the budget itself. the core of this is cooperation for peace and the protection of human dignity from the beginning to the end of life. i voted for the budget as a whole, and i voted for all the changes which, as far as i can see, lead to a greater focus on the core areas of the eu.
in writing. the commission is also proposing to extend the scope of the directive to include the use of the term 'competent authority' to cover all the activities of the european parliament, including the provision of information on the activities of the european parliament and the commission. instead of prioritising savings, the commission and the majority of parliament have opted to demand increased spending and the introduction of eu taxes. this increase is not justifiable because of the lack of priorities and the considerable savings that the majority of member states are making. the introduction of new own resources does not reduce the burden on citizens. we help solve europe's problems by using the eu and its member states' revenues efficiently and by prioritising the most important spending. large subsidies to agriculture do not contribute to growth and jobs, but investments in innovation and research do. the long-term budget proposal lacks priorities to contribute aggressively to growth and competitiveness. the eu's long-term budget must be modernised and made more efficient. outdated expenditure items need to be revised and money put into growth-generating areas such as research and development. cohesion policy is concentrated on encouraging research, the development of new businesses and infrastructure. member states face difficult budgetary challenges that require priorities, and those priorities must also be made at eu level. it is against this background that we have not given our support to the report.
in writing. - in view of the economic situation in the member states, we believe that this should be done within the framework of a budget not exceeding the current level. for example, we believe that agricultural subsidies should be cut more than in the commission's proposal and that a redistribution should be made to the structural funds and environmental and climate investments. we also do not believe that new sources of revenue should be added to the eu budget and we do not believe that sweden's share of the funding should be increased. given that the report has good content on growth, jobs and solidarity, but lacks sufficient ambition for savings and reform, we have decided not to vote on the final text.
in writing. - we are convinced that the eu is needed to tackle common challenges such as climate change, organised crime and the humane treatment of asylum seekers in europe. we also believe that a strong eu and a well-functioning internal market are needed to get europe's economy back on track. we have therefore voted in favour of increasing investment in actions contributing to the europe 2020 strategy, such as research and entrepreneurship, and against maintaining the budget for the eu's agricultural policy. we believe that the eu's objectives and tasks should be achieved within the framework of the european commission's proposals, through clear priorities and strategic cuts - not through an increased budget. that is why we voted against the european globalisation adjustment fund, for example, and for the european parliament's travelling circus between brussels and strasbourg to be stopped. we also call for a more coherent and transparent eu budget. we believe that politics is about choosing, and choosing also means setting priorities. in these times of economic crisis in the eu member states, it would be irresponsible to demand an increased eu budget.
in writing. − the eu budget should be used for what can bring european added value. it is one of the pillars of the budgetary process and is also the one that will guide the distribution of the common funds at the disposal of the union. so far, everything is fine. the interim report voted on in plenary on 23 october stresses and attaches great importance to new own resources for the eu. this means the financial transaction tax, which i firmly argue is a waste of money for sweden and europe. own resources too often mean a tax to be paid directly to the eu budget. more money for the eu in the form of own resources is not automatically an investment that will help europe out of its troubles and strains. instead, we need to prioritise the resources now available. it is important to recall that a tobin tax has been tried in sweden and proved harmful. transactions are moved from sweden. it is also important to emphasise - as a matter of course - that the cost of ftt is borne by the average consumer and that economic growth is being hampered. since so much emphasis has been placed on own resources and the demand for a financial transaction tax, i chose to vote against the report.
in writing. - we welcome the review and simplification of the extremely complex vat revenue for the eu budget. we also welcome the reduction in the administrative burden on the member states. however, since the proposal concerns the taxation field, we do not think that the methods of collecting such a modified vat revenue should be regulated in a regulation. the proposal should instead be dealt with in the own resources decision, which requires unanimity in the council and ratification by all member states.
you guys! the additional protocol between the european union and israel on bilateral cooperation is mutually beneficial and of great importance. this will improve access to low-cost, patent-free medical devices by removing technical barriers. israeli medicines are among the best in the world and are used by many medical institutions, including most european health care institutions. the protocol will speed up the lengthy certification process, reducing costs and improving access to medicines, allowing patients to pay less. this is particularly important in times of crisis for every citizen. the highest quality control of medicines required by the european regulatory authorities will be maintained. the protocol also opens up possibilities for european manufacturers of medicinal products to export to israel. when we talk about supporting innovation, it would provide real support for the larger innovation sector - pharma. and so will jobs and growth. the protocol would also serve as a first step in other industrial sectors to harmonise european and israeli legislation. the introduction of a uniform certification system could reduce the cost of importing patent-free medicines from israel by up to €10 billion per year, but the new certification procedures will ensure that only registered and eu-approved israeli companies can produce medicines for export. this will ensure that only the best medicines tested reach the european market. on the other hand, the production time of the companies that will be subject to the one-off certification procedure will be reduced to up to three years. the economic benefits of the protocol are clear. there is another benefit, supporting a democratic regime in this region of conflict and violence. and i can only agree with mr caspary that if we have a deal with palestine, then we have to do the same with israel. i call on my colleagues to support the adoption of the protocol without delay. thank you.
mr president, i would like to thank you. it would be interesting to compare this debate on the trade agreement with israel with debates on other trade agreements around the world. it has been said that this was to be some sort of gift or reward for benjamin netanyahu. that is still underestimating the eu's own ability to trade and a strange way of reasoning, is it not? we know that today, here in europe, medicines are prescribed in unprecedented quantities from israel. they're talking about 3.5 million a day. we also know that these medicines are cheaper, as was said recently in this house. it is not entirely wrong if the eu can get something cheaper and of the highest quality in terms of fighting type 2 diabetes and multiple sclerosis and lowering blood pressure. someone in this house said that when we talk about china, we always talk about human rights. yes, although i haven't heard anyone say we should cut off trade with china. well, then, there would be a consequence. we cannot talk about occupied territories unless we dare to mention why these territories are occupied and why israel does not live within the 1967 borders. i think it is an enormous simplification to forget it if we are to bring it up in this debate, which would not be necessary at all but has been done by so many people. so this agreement was approved two and a half years ago and now it is being mixed up and violated in this way. it would be straightforward and honest to talk about why you're messing up because it's an absurdity. we are instead infecting the possibilities of influence in the middle east, and that is not what the european parliament was supposed to want.
mr president, i would like to thank you. to make the caa a pawn in a grand political game about the middle east, as the social democrats and the left here in the european parliament do, is both irresponsible and a betrayal of the sick in israel and europe who need medicines. it is wrong to say that this agreement is about upgrading trade relations between the eu and israel. the agreement is not about upgraded relations or the israeli-palestinian conflict. it is about facilitating the introduction and approval of new medicines. everything to make sick in israel and in europe should have access to the best medicine, as cheaply as possible and as quickly as possible. to stop this agreement is to punish those who we should agree are completely innocent of any guilt in this conflict, namely the sick. we started this work back in 2010 and we were working in parallel with a fruit and vegetable agreement with palestine. in 2011, i voted to approve the trade agreement with palestine on fruit and vegetables. we did it in parallel, just because we didn't want to send any political signals one way or the other. but the left here stopped the agreement with israel and only approved the agreement with palestine. it's making a big political game out of something that shouldn't be a big political game. i would like to conclude by saying that the sick in europe should not pay the price for the left's attempt to make political points out of things which could actually make life easier for people in israel and europe.

the rapporteur. - mr president! - what is it? i will not use all my speaking time now, but i may wish to return at the end of the debate if it is possible to answer some questions. first of all, i would like to point out that the purpose of this report is to adapt our legislation to the changes which have come into force as a result of the european parliament's co-decision in trade matters under the new treaty. it is basically a rather technical matter to speed up the procedure and ensure that we do not take an unusually long time. to do this, the commission has stated that we need delegated acts to both achieve predictability and ensure that the european parliament does not sit with long lists of products and go through them one by one. there are some of these issues that should rather be dealt with by experts in delegated acts and that we could then examine. the challenge here is that we have to be quite quick in processing this case. if we do not, we will have old lists of so-called dual-use items covering only the system changes that have taken place up to 2010. the case we are dealing with now needs to be dealt with fairly quickly in order to cover all the changes that have taken place in 2011. when we discussed this in committee, we had a little too much conflict, unfortunately, and in a rather unnecessary way. a great many comments were made which were not on the technical issue of how we should work with delegated acts and get a faster and more efficient handling where i think we are basically in agreement, myself and the shadow rapporteur. many other issues were raised and discussed as well. i understand from the socialist group, among others, that you want to table some amendments again. this applies, for example, to proposals on cybersecurity, transparency, etc. i just want to point out that i agree with several of them and i think several of them are relevant. but i do not want to bring them up here and risk delaying the adoption of this document by referring to the amendments proposed by the socialist group, among others. we know that there is going to be a major overhaul of the whole system of dual-use products. the commission plans to present something in 2013 and a legislative proposal will come in 2014. i therefore see no urgent reason why we should have to implement the changes which i know have been proposed by the socialist group, among others. i therefore hope that we can now take this technical matter and make it easier and then, at a later stage, return to many of the relevant points made by my colleagues. i hope that we can now deal with this particular matter quickly and efficiently, in accordance with the committee's proposal.
member of the commission. − mr president, i will be very brief. i just wanted to assure the european parliament that we are committed to providing all relevant information and documents to the european parliament when preparing delegated acts, so as to ensure appropriate scrutiny of delegated acts, in line with our institutional commitments. secondly, on what mrs schaake has been saying about software and so on, she should acknowledge that the eu has taken swift and robust action to prevent the export of telecommunications interception and internet monitoring equipment in violation of human rights. this was, in particular, the case with the adoption of sanctions against syria and iran. having said this, what this proposal is about is speed: speed when something should be removed from the list; speed also when something should be put on the list. that is what we are asking the house’s agreement for.
rapporteur. − mr president, first of all we do not disagree on the most substantial issues. these dual-use items could be used to oppress people and could be used for war, and therefore we have – or may i say we should have – the strongest legislation in the world to control these items. the problem is that we do not have this any more since, because of the lisbon treaty, our procedures for changing these lists are too cumbersome and take too long. as the world is changing, we have to be swift and effective in changing legislation. that is exactly why we need to swiftly adopt this legislation instead of pre-empting the whole debate over changing the legislation. that debate has been scheduled, so let us address that issue when we get there. but now we need to update this legislation on the basis of the commission proposal. so each thing at its right time – now it is time to adopt this piece of legislation and not to pre-empt future debates.

in writing. - we voted today in favour of eight reports on the use of the european globalisation adjustment fund. since the fund was set up in 2006, we have always abstained from voting because we were against the fund from the beginning, as we do not believe that the fund is the right instrument to help workers affected by structural changes. the fund has now grown, evolved and received its own allocation. it exists, and it will not disappear for the foreseeable future. we have therefore now decided to take a case-by-case view of whether it meets the very strict requirements for co-financing and long-term employment measures imposed on applicants. in these cases, we considered that the applications fulfilled these requirements. we will continue to support more effective and long-term instruments in the framework of budgetary and long-term budgetary work to increase employment, respond to structural change and increase labour market flexibility.

for the alde group. mr president, i would like to thank you for your reply. thank you, commissioner. i would also like to thank markus ferber for his successful work. this is a very technically complicated bill and he has managed to unite parliament on well-balanced compromises. these two bills are the foundation for restoring some of the confidence that was lost in the financial crash four years ago. we are tightening the rules, as i said, on high-frequency trading, and not everyone is completely satisfied. braking systems must be in place to reduce the risk of an uncontrolled stock market crash. we are increasing competition and transparency in the market by setting up a new trading platform, as the commissioner mentioned. we are making sure that small bond markets - and here i am a bit nationalistic - that the swedish get reasonable terms. we are strengthening the protection of savers by tightening the rules on the sale of savings products, as the rapporteur suggested. we are achieving, and this is important to me, greater freedom of choice, better competition and a more robust and better functioning capital market. mr president, the question of the prohibition of commissions, of inducements, has been the most difficult to agree on. self-cleaning and ethical guidelines have clearly not been sufficient to reduce aggressive sales of savings products. and here i admit as a liberal, a market liberal, that there is a problem. i admit that, mr goebbels. a general eu ban on commissions is the next step unless the industry takes a more serious stance. but we in the liberal group do not want to see a total ban on commissions throughout the eu today. the countries that want to have stricter rules, like the uk or the netherlands, are allowed to have them. the risk of a total ban is that the sale will result in those with lower incomes no longer being offered the free counselling they need. small insurance intermediaries risk being knocked out. mr president, financial advice must be available to all citizens at a reasonable cost. nor must competition and supply be reduced. transparency and openness are of paramount importance. it is an important part of ensuring greater protection for purchasers of savings products. i would like to thank mr ferber and all the shadow rapporteurs.
mr president, it is important to recognise that there are no stronger arguments for reform than in those member states that have lost competitiveness and in those economic sectors that lack competitiveness. that reform can never be forced upon member states, but there must be an understanding that reform is needed. increased deficits or increased spending or – referring to the debate we have had – a lower pension age do not solve any problems. we have seen, on the other hand, that the deficits and debt burdens are undermining not only public finances but also the competitiveness of eu member states. it is important to state that we do have a common commitment to making the reforms which are needed, but these must be based on political will and democratic support in each member state. i do not think we, from the european union, should regulate labour markets, because that must be left up to the different member states. however, i do think we need to ensure, and place emphasis on, a commitment to reforms which can guarantee that we have a functioning and dynamic single market that turns the world’s biggest economy into the world’s biggest and most dynamic market. this underscores the need for reforms to the single market to be accompanied by market reforms in the member states. i would like us to have a horizontal approach regarding the single market and market reforms, but also a vertical one, in each member state. this will facilitate, and ensure there is an opening-up to more competition, more plurality and more competitiveness. that should be the next step forward in the european semester – or whatever it is to be called – in the future. in order to do this we will need a commitment to making our european economies more competitive, and that is the task for us.
mr president, i would like to thank you. my impression is that the european semester is working better and better. at the same time, there are problems that need to be addressed. a key problem is the commission's tendency to encroach on the autonomy of the social partners in its country-specific recommendations. in this year's recommendations, the commission calls on a number of countries to restructure their national wage formation systems and to reduce national wage levels. the commission's behaviour is not acceptable. full respect for the autonomy of the parties is essential for the proper functioning of a labour market. furthermore, any attack on autonomy is contrary to fundamental trade union freedoms and rights. autonomy is enshrined in the ilo convention, the treaty on the functioning of the european union and the charter of fundamental rights of the european union. in this parliament we have recently clearly defended this autonomy in the positions taken on six-pack and two-pack. it is therefore clear that the commission must change its course on this issue.
the s&d group is in favour of the proposal. mr president, i would like to thank you for your reply. i would like to thank in particular the rapporteur, mr eider gardiazábal, who has done a great deal of work on this very difficult subject of the future. it has turned out to be a fantastically good report. the economic future of europe cannot be a matter of emergency measures to deal with the crisis. it is just as important to stimulate the economy in the long term as it is to increase growth-generating investment. the crisis is not only a consequence of the banking and financial collapse but has also been aggravated by a long-term decline in investment. it has gotten worse since the crisis, and now we are down to dangerously low levels of investment. at the same time, as the rapporteur pointed out, the investment needs are enormous. the problem is that with the current severe restrictions on the eu budget and on national budgets, the tightening of capital requirements on banks, and so on, we are not getting any money, but we have to find new ways forward. then there are financial instruments, including project bonds, which we agreed to test during a pilot phase this summer, based on my own report, among other things. in this context, we will ensure that, through eib loans guaranteed by the eu budget, project companies can issue bonds that are more secure and attractive, so that pension funds, for example, can invest in infrastructure projects instead of leaving money in different accounts. i am very pleased that the commissioner is now announcing that concrete projects are already being put on the table and contracts are being signed. the idea is to evaluate the pilot phase. we will see if we can raise even more money in the coming years and maybe also try other models with even more potential. i believe, however, that if we are to take europe out of austerity and recession back to sustainable growth and job creation, we need these new financial instruments.
